### 4.1 Wave 0: interfaces (two coders, about 2-3 days: stream A, and stream R's coder on R0)

- [x] A1 Witness block codec, version `PW 01` (spec 16): encode, decode, exact lengths, nothing trailing.
- [x] A2 `M` codec (spec 6): roots, masters, settles.
- [x] A3 Canonical encodings (spec 17): values; argument pushes with the minimal-push rule; `argsHash`; results;
  addresses as locking bytecode.
- [x] A4 `pin(cat)`, `addrV(cat)` and `H_V` helpers (spec R5, 4a, N1).
- [x] A5 Stop-site metadata schema in the artifact (spec 18): reads with their record, calls with their index,
  segment closes.
- [x] A6 Frame and call kind constants, and the record entry convention `(in, out)` (spec 4a, 16).
- [x] A7 Golden vectors:
  - the `ABBA` witness blocks, `M` and outputs (spec 5.1-5.3, 6);
  - `argsHash` cases, including the minimal-push traps;
  - `addrV` for sample categories;
  - one malformed block per T33 case.
- [x] A8 The interface freeze is recorded in the Log (version and vector hashes).
- A9 (size budget) moved to Phase R, after M2 (D3).
- [x] R0 Reuse proof for records (stream R's coder, in parallel with A: it reads only today's code). Focused tests
  showing that today's sorted-list and covenant helpers (`SortedList.ts`, `covenant-helpers.ts`, `state.ts`)
  implement the semantics spec section 20 assumes: exact read; absence by strict predecessor; insert with a relinked
  predecessor; update in place; delete as the default value; composite keys for nested mappings; the 60/64-byte value
  thresholds. Any gap found is written into spec section 20 before Wave 1.

### 4.2 Wave 1: core streams (B, C, R, E in parallel)

**Stream B: validator** (spec 3.2, 7.2). Its tests use hand-built transactions with stub masters (small scripts
holding minting NFTs, whose unlocking bytecode carries the A7 blocks), so B needs no compiler work.
- [x] B1 V1: recreation in place; a master of its own category present.
- [ ] B2 V2 (partial): parse `M` and every covered master's first-push witness block; reject any encoding the master
  would not accept. Emitted: the wire name and version, the root, master and settled-state indices, the witness-block
  shape and the empty settle tail. Not emitted: the remaining encoding rules.
- [x] B3 V3: completeness.
- [x] B4 V4: roots, and the forest rule.
- [ ] B5 V5 (partial): edges, for each kind. Emitted for an ordinary call: caller, kind, origin, value, sender,
  address, result, and the callee's own pushes hashed against the recorded digest. Not emitted: the `DELEGATE` target
  rule and the `INTERNAL` same-contract rule.
  - `CALL`, `STATIC`, `CREATE`: sender is the caller's `self`, and `self` is the recorded target;
  - `DELEGATE`: passthrough, and the callee's category matches the target (T37);
  - `INTERNAL`: passthrough, same contract;
  - for all kinds: origin, values (passed through for `DELEGATE` and `INTERNAL`), `retval`, and `argsHash` over the
    callee's argument pushes.
- [x] B6 V6 (ticked 2026-09-28): every master visited exactly once, so cycles and unreachable masters are refused,
  and the ordering half is derived on chain: a recursive depth-first visit gathers each settled contract's record
  entries in the order the call tree gives, and the chains rule (B7) follows them in it, so a callee runs between
  its caller's segments and roots never interleave (T9, T30).
- [x] B7 V7 (ticked 2026-09-27; since 2026-09-28 the chains are followed in the V6 order rather than order-free, which
  retires the greedy walk and its refusals of branching chains, and the 400-case differential agrees): every settlement
  rule the program lists is held against the model over generated
  compositions and pinned by a machine refusal of its own — no category settled twice
  (`emitSettleCategoriesAreDistinct`),
  the state-inputs half (`emitSettlementStateInputs`: every input the transcript lists as a settled contract's state
  is a mutable NFT of its category and not the validator, every such input in the transaction is listed, and no index
  twice), the records the frames read against the state UTXOs the contract spends (`2cb452941`), the census
  (`d055ac85f`, `abe0de286`: no own-category fungible amount, a mutable own-category output only for a record a frame
  declares, a bare own-category output only for an event a frame lists inside the transaction and no event listed
  twice, and every master of the contract recreated at its own index), the value floor (`82879cfcc`) and the record
  chains (`ccde697cc`, this round for the end), both halves: an entry continues from the transaction's own commitment
  at the record's input or from another declaration's end view, and is continued by another declaration's start view
  or by the transaction's own commitment at the record's output, with a declaration that creates the record
  continuing from nothing and one that deletes it continued by nothing. The census reads
  `OP_OUTPUTTOKENCATEGORY` rather than the commitment because the opcode appends a capability byte only for mutable
  and minting tokens, so an immutable NFT of a settled category and a fungible token of it are the same 32 bytes on
  the wire (the Log row and Deviations carry the measurement). The chains are both halves: the pairing above, and the
  chain's end decided without the order by an anchor — every record entry carries a marker byte, one when the
  declaration's write lands at the transaction's own output or when it deletes the record, and an entry passes only
  if its key has a marked declaration anywhere in the blob. That is what puts `V7_CHAIN_END_MISMATCH` in the list, and
  the rule is checked both ways: with the anchor search neutered the differential misses `seed 24301 case 55` and
  with it the corpus is green. **Unticked again the same day: the pairing and the anchor are not connectivity.** Two
  read-only declarations of one record that both claim the same view pair with each other, and the record's honest
  write satisfies the anchor, so the program accepts a chain that never reaches the spent commitment; the model
  refuses it with `V7_CHAIN_BROKEN` (the Log row and the Deviations carry the repro and the rule the fix needs). Not
  in the program's list, and outside this item's scope: `V7_MASTER_NOT_COVERED` (carried by V3's master-set rule),
  `V7_RECORD_OUTPUT_NOT_A_RECORD` and `V7_SEND_OUTPUT_OUT_OF_RANGE`.
- [x] B8 V8: the settles set (two or more masters, or a `DELEGATE` or `INTERNAL` frame on the records). Both halves
  are emitted and each has its own refusal; the branch for the borrowed half was written but not called until
  `b52b7ec5b`, which an end-to-end refusal test caught.
- [x] B9 V9: owner policy.
- [x] B10 V10: static frames.
- [x] B11 V11: addresses, with `addrV` built from `OP_ACTIVEBYTECODE` (matches the A4 vectors).
- [x] B12 The `ABBA` golden transaction, with stub masters carrying the A7 blocks, is accepted. Verified
  green 2026-09-27: `validator/script.test.ts` "accepts the reference composition at the validator input" runs the
  shipped program over `abbaComposition()`, and `validator/golden.test.ts` derives `V1`-`V11` accepted in the
  section-8 order.
- [x] B13 One hostile unit test per V-check. Each rule the program emits has its own refusal in
  `validator/script.test.ts` (43 of them), beside `validator/hostile.test.ts` and the machine-level suite in
  `hostile/`.
- [x] B14 Reference model and differential tests. An independent TypeScript model of V1-V11, written from the spec
  text rather than from the script. A property-based harness generates valid and adversarial compositions (mutated
  witnesses, `M`, orders, record chains, edges) and requires the script and the model to agree on every accept and
  reject. B13 proves each check exists; B14 checks that the derived order and the record chaining agree with the
  spec over graphs nobody wrote by hand.
- [x] B15 No master-lock assumption in `V_TEMPLATE`. Masters are identified by category and minting capability only
  (V1, V3, V7); the census keeps every minting NFT at its master lock. This keeps the loader form (spec 12) open
  after the freeze. Checked by B14's model and one vector whose masters sit at a loader lock.

**Stream C: frames and calls** (spec 4a, spec R3, and spec 7.1: spec P1-P2, W0-W3, O1, N1).
- [x] C1 Option `composition: 'per-call'` and its single entry hook (a declared hook commit).
- [x] C2 spec P1: category pin; spec P2: recreation in place.
- [x] C3 W0: the first-push self-check.
- [x] C4 W1: frames.
  - `callerRef != self`.
  - `ROOT`: a P2PKH caller, hash type `0x41` or `0x61`, and `sender`.
  - `self` from the baked `addrX` (`ROOT`, `CALL`, `STATIC`, `CREATE`) or from the witness (`DELEGATE`,
    `INTERNAL`).
  - `msg.value = value`.
- [x] C5 O1: `tx.origin` from `originRef`, with the hash-type check; never the first-P2PKH scan.
- [x] C6 N1: the need test, and validator recognition through `H_V`.
- [x] C7 W2: calls. Kind and target recorded, `calleeRef != self`, in-script `argsHash` with minimal pushes (A3),
  result from the witness, segment closed at every call. Complete as of `0632ecdc6`: the call data's own order is
  now the order the digest is folded in, so a call with more than one argument is agreed on by the caller, the
  callee's body and the validator. The clauses landed over the call-site commits before it; this box is ticked by
  the commit that made the wire facts hold for any arity.
- [ ] C8 spec R3 call forms:
  - internal calls and `delegatecall` to own code, inline;
  - `this.g()` inline with `msg.sender = self`, replacing the `AstBuilder.ts:7323` behaviour under per-call. Landed
    (`0e70219d6`): the caller hands the callee its own contract address as the callee's sender and address, so
    `msg.sender == address(this)` holds inside the self-called body and a caller-gated function reached that way is
    refused; the base-cast spelling agrees. `msg.value` inside a value-less self call is unchanged.
  - a runtime target equal to self as a `CALL`. Landed: a call through an interface value whose run-time target is
    the caller's own address (`IFoo(address(this)).m()`, `IFoo(this).m()`) is classified at its site as the value
    the caller's own code holds — its frame `self` — so the record's `target` is bound to it as it is for any
    computed target, and the callee runs on a second master of the same contract with `msg.sender == address(this)`,
    the same identity the inline `this.g()` presents. A target that is another contract is refused at the caller.
  - `STATIC` for `view` and `pure`;
  - `try` must succeed (T22).
- [ ] C9 W3: events at their listed outputs; `sends[]` for P2PKH value sends; a value send to a contract as a `CALL`.
- [x] C10 Stop-site metadata emitted (A5). The per-call compiler derives each entry's sites from the calls it
  already compiles at their call sites and the segments they split the execution into — one `call` site per external
  call in execution order, then one `segmentClose` per segment, so `k` calls publish `2k + 1` sites — validates the
  table against the frozen schema before it is published, and attaches it to `artifact.composition`. The source-side
  facts a driver resolves a callee through (interface, method, target, selector) ride beside each call site, and no
  `read` site is published: the compiler does not hold the record identity a read resolves to, and an invented one
  would be worse than an omitted one.
- [x] C11 A code constant slot for `addrX`; stream F fills it at deployment.
- [ ] C12 SafeERC20 low-level calls (OpenZeppelin 5.0.2 and 4.9.3) lowered to typed calls; the `code.length` rule.
  **Landed for the payloads the compiler can name** (`907cd7ed3`). A low-level call whose payload statically names
  an interface method — `address(t).call(abi.encodeCall(I.f, (args)))` or the `abi.encodeWithSelector(I.f.selector,
  args)` spelling, in the result-binding, result-ignoring and `return` positions — is rewritten before semantic
  analysis into the typed call `I(t).f(args)`, so the call record, the argument digest and the callee reference come
  from the same code path a written typed call uses; the low-level result is rebuilt as `success = true` and
  `returndata = abi.encode(<f's return values>)`, so `abi.decode(data, (bool))` round-trips. Measured: the derived
  call record matches the written typed call on every field and every contract input accepts
  (`edges/low-level-token-call-lowering.test.ts`). **Still refused by name**: a payload held in a local (`bytes data
  = abi.encodeCall(...); address(t).call(data)` — the vendored 4.9.3 `Address.functionCall` indirection, pinned in
  `shapes/proxied-root-append.test.ts`), a run-time selector, an opaque `bytes` payload, `abi.encodeCall(this.f, …)`
  and a method no known interface names. The `code.length` gate is served through the general P2SH-only extractor,
  so a plain-account or zero-address target fails the script rather than answering zero (that boundary is pinned).
  `address(this).balance` constant-folded for value 0 (spec 17; section 23 rows 9-11).
- [ ] C13 `ecrecover` returns address 0 for invalid input (spec 23 row 22). **Measured, and still not met**
  (`b382ff160` pinned it; `aac352885` and `a91a6c396` moved the per-call path from refusing every such body to
  serving it). The compiler now admits and emits a body that calls `ecrecover` and reads the co-spent pair at the
  master's own input plus `1 + 2i`/`2 + 2i`, and the composer places those inputs, builds them from the call's own
  argument pushes through the shipped witness and side-car builders, and shifts every later participant. Measured on
  the machine: a valid `ecrecoverPubKey` is accepted at the master and both side-car inputs, a valid `ecrecoverEvm`
  at the master and all three, and a recovery whose key is not the source's is rejected by the master's own compare.
  **Neither convention implements the zero-address rule**, and the per-call measurement now says why: the pair
  commits whatever key its witnessed `(hash, v, r, s)` recovers, so a `v` outside 27/28 cannot be split at all, an
  out-of-range `s` recovers a non-matching key, and the all-zero digest cannot be witnessed (`Cannot find square
  root`). A recovery call whose argument the code computes is served (`101cb6131`), and so is a delegate frame's
  side-car placement (after its own input, not the records' owner); a loop over one stays open;
  `master/ecrecover-invalid-input.test.ts` pins each input shape beside a direct-path control.
- [ ] C14 Fix: a public function that reads `msg.sender` and writes state fails `OP_VERIFY` when called internally
  (spec open point 9).
- [x] C15 Refusal is per body. A contract whose one function is a shape the master cannot serve still compiles: the
  bodies it can serve are emitted, the refused body is emitted as a body that fails whenever it is reached, and the
  fact that refuses it is published by name on `artifact.composition.refusedEntries`, so a builder reports which
  function is unsupported instead of the whole contract. The refusal closes over invocations, so an entry that runs
  a refused body is refused with it and named with the body it runs. Contract-wide stays contract-wide: a shape the
  head or the tail fixes (no entry point, a constructor the head cannot share, a fallback, contract creation,
  function pointers, inline assembly) refuses the unit rather than a body. A layout question is asked per body too: a
  body whose own layout places the dynamic-value chunk run at a width the head's one coordinate does not address is
  refused by name, and the master fixes the width most of its bodies use.

**Stream R: records and settlement, view mode** (spec 20, spec 7.1 checks spec S1-S4, spec 7.3). This is the main
risk. Take the slices in order, each with its own tests.
- [x] R1 Data NFTs: reads from `viewIn`, and writes compared with `viewOut` at segment close, reusing today's record
  encoders. A body now reads its own segment's declared view rather than the commitment the transaction spends:
  `declared-view.ts` walks the witness block from the body (stepping the call records and earlier segments by their
  declared widths) and leaves the view of the record at the coordinate it is given, failing instead of falling back
  when a segment declares nothing there; a source read and the close both go through it; a value written before an
  external call is marked as the closed segment's so a read after the call resolves from the declaration; and the
  end-of-body rebuild closes each record against the segment the frame last touched it in. The worked shape where
  `A`'s one record is written by three frames now derives end to end and every contract input is accepted, with four
  controls refused (a frame whose computed commitment is not its segment's `viewOut`, one whose read is not its
  segment's `viewIn`, a standalone master whose last declaration is not the transaction's output, and a settlement
  whose later segment does not carry on from the one before it). Turning the declared read off fails seven of that
  file's ten cases and dropping the call-site marking fails five. Remaining: the close is emitted where the
  end-of-body rebuild runs, so a frame that writes a record early and leaves it untouched later does not prove that
  early value against that segment's declaration; a record whose index changes between segments is not followed; and
  dynamic chunks still resolve against the transaction.
- [x] R2 Self-settlement, spec S1-S4: chains, census, the input rule (spec S3), values (ticked 2026-09-27, when the
  chains rule stopped admitting a detached cycle; see B7). The spec's state rules D1
  and D2 in `STATE_CODE`, reusing today's data-NFT covenant with an added value pin. Each rule is enforced on both
  paths: the master head settles the records its own frame declares against the segment views it walks from its own
  block (`58e8f7ee3`, `c44994de8`), and the validator enforces the contract's settlement across the frames that run
  on its records — the chains, the census, the touched-records input rule and the value floor, each with a machine
  refusal and the differential over generated compositions (`ccde697cc`, and this round for the chain end). The
  master-side controls are in `solidc/test/per-call/master/record-settlement.test.ts`, `settlement-walk.test.ts` and
  `mapping-delete-write.test.ts`; the composed shape is `solidcash/test/per-call/frames/round-trip-stateful.test.ts`,
  whose `refuses a standalone master whose last declaration is not the transaction's output` and
  `refuses the settlement when a later segment does not carry on from the one before it` are the S1 and S2 refusals.
  The gaps the emitted body still has are the ones R1's own text records (the close is emitted at the end-of-body
  rebuild rather than at each intermediate segment close, an index that changes between segments is not followed,
  and dynamic chunks still resolve against the transaction).
- [ ] R3 Mapping nodes:
  - exact read, and absence proof by predecessor;
  - update in place;
  - insert: a created record plus a relinked predecessor;
  - delete as the default value: driven end to end at `07c80be46`. A source-level `delete m[k]` lowers to the
    upsert of the value type's default, so the published access is a write and the frame's record group is the same
    write's — the node kept in place for an update, the relinked predecessor and the created node for an insert —
    and the machine accepts both through the shipped compile, deploy and derivation
    (`solidc/test/per-call/master/mapping-delete-write.test.ts`). A record declaring the node removed, as a splice
    would, is refused, so the case drives the settlement rather than the encoding;
  - nodes created and relinked across frames, and the creation a **constructor** performs. A per-call contract
    whose constructor writes one sorted-mapping entry now derives its genesis: the write's key and value are resolved
    from the constructor's own arguments, the nodes are planned with the shipped `planMappingWrite` against
    `headSentinelCommitment()`, the spent head sentinel and the created node are placed in the probe program and
    minted at the collection's entry covenant, and the constructor frame is run for real so its record and its
    entries are the commitments the shipped planner computes
    (`deploy/genesis.test.ts`; `planConstructorMappingWrite` in `deploy/genesis.ts`, `entryRecordLockingBytecode` in
    `deploy/records.ts`). Mutating the created node's value or the relinked head's successor is refused, and a
    constructor writing two collections is refused by name.
  - **`classifyPerCallDeployment` has no mapping-node bucket.** The live UTXOs of a contract that keeps a mapping
    are its nodes: a mutable commitment whose first 32 bytes are a slot and whose tail is the value, which does not
    parse as a data-NFT, so `nftIndexOf` answers `undefined` and the node is classified as a **validator**. Nothing
    that reads a contract's pools today is misled — the genesis and `readPerCallState` read data-NFT records by
    index — but a composition that re-derives pool membership would see a mapping contract's nodes as validators. The
    smallest change is to hand the classifier the collection signatures and match a mutable commitment's leading slot
    against them (and slot 0 against the head sentinel's shape) before the validator fallback.
  - **A frame that keeps a data-NFT record and writes a mapping batch settles for a frame that owns its records**
    (two fixes: `073632a9d` and the round whose Log row follows it). The batch derivation read its target groups from
    the head of the segment's record list while a segment declares the frame's own state records first, so the walk
    split a state commitment as a node (`OP_SPLIT`, `requested split index: 32`); it now steps over those records and
    offsets a created node's declared position by the same count. Every state and entry coordinate of a per-call
    frame is then based on the input the head parked as the records' owner, so a frame that runs on its caller's
    records resolves them where the caller's block put them. A user-entered root whose entry keeps its record, writes
    two mapping keys (an update and an insert) and emits now derives, is accepted at every contract input, and its
    writes read back as the shipped planner computed them (`shapes/record-with-mapping-write.test.ts`, with a record
    output no frame computed refused by the validator), and the same entry behind a proxy hop settles too: the proof
    operand a batch derivation leaves is measured from the input the frame's records are addressed from, which for a
    `DELEGATE` frame is its caller's input, so the reader and the derivation agree and the declared reads resolve
    (`shapes/proxied-registration.test.ts`, with a created node no frame computed refused by the validator). This is
    what the marketplace's registering entries and the first flow shape's registration half need.
  - The read of the entry a write targets is derived from the same group as of `3d432f7a9`: an access that reads
    and writes one target is admitted, and the machine accepts both its present and its absent case
    (`solidc/test/per-call/master/mapping-read-write.test.ts`). A read of an entry the execution does not write
    stays refused, since the frame fixes no value for it.
- [ ] R4 Long mapping values: the value arrives as a witness bound by the root, and each writing frame mints its
  version's chunks. Both halves are served: the read in `8e67cfaaf` and the write, its chunks declared and
  counted, in the row the Log gives it; a read-modify-write of one long value in one frame has no case yet.
  **Measured, and it splits into three pieces rather than one.** The per-call head derives exactly one dynamic
  coordinate (`__internal__dynamicChunkInputBase`) and nothing for the *value-witness* family: a
  `mapping(string => string)` write carries four hidden operands (`__internal__valueWitness_0`,
  `__internal__leadingPushValue_0`, `__internal__valueChunkOutputBase`, `__internal__completePushValue_0`). For a
  **write-only** access they are dead — stripping them compiles a 21,314-byte master that reads none of them — but
  any access that **reads** the node's value (a read-modify-write, or an array push into it, which is the
  marketplace's own `jobEvents[jobId].push`) needs the witness itself, and the **witness-block wire format has no
  variable-length value field**: supplying it takes either a wire-format change (version bump, validator V2,
  composer, machine) or materializing the value on chain from co-spent chunks, which the emitted body does not do.
  The composer is missing the other half — `planMappingWrite`/`layOutMappingWrites` place a node with a
  fixed-width tail, and the shipped chunk planner is for dynamic *state variables* while a long mapping value uses
  the root-addressed **value-chunk** form (`splitValueIntoValueChunks`, `buildValueChunkCommitment`,
  `valueChunkSignature`) that nothing lays out; the emitted body requires exactly `ceil(size/126)` value-chunk NFTs
  at the derived address (`emitSortedValueChunkPin`), so a long write would be refused by the machine rather than
  by name. **The delete half is served.** Codegen already lowered a sorted delete to an upsert of the value type's
  default (`visitMappingDelete`, because `sortedValueKind('string') === 'rawbytes'` is non-null); the per-call
  admission now counts a `delete` as the one write it is (`isOneWrite`, scoped there so the kernel paths that read
  `isSortedValue` are untouched), and the composer gained `planMappingDelete`, which plans that same transition —
  the node rewritten in place with the caller's encoded default, `removed: false` — instead of the splice removal
  the `value: undefined` form plans. Measured: `Tags.remove(string k) { delete this.tags[k]; }` compiles with no
  refusal and no value-witness operand, the node's output commitment is `encodeEntryCommitment(slot, ZERO_SLOT,
  encodeChunkableValueTail(empty))`, every contract input is accepted, and the **set** stays refused by name for the
  value witness (`shapes/string-mapping-delete.test.ts`). A **long** value is not covered: the delete writes the
  short empty default, so no value chunks are involved. And a `put(string, string)` master that emits at all is 21,314 bytes, past the 10,000-byte P2SH per-input
  limit, so this shape needs the code split on a real chain whatever else is done (D3).
  **The read half and the write-only set are served, in the v1 block** (this row's commit). As the user ruled,
  the format is reworked in place rather than versioned, since nothing is released: the block keeps `"PW" 01` and
  gains a trailing value list, `valueCount:u16 { value:lp32 }` (spec section 16), so every block grows by two bytes
  and the interface vectors were regenerated. The head parks one slot per long-value read of the contract's widest
  body, empty past the listed values and refusing a longer list, and the body checks each value against its node's
  root; the direct convention's fold operands (`leadingPushValue_N`, `completePushValue_N`,
  `valueChunkOutputBase`) are stripped, since a per-call body neither receives nor reads them. The validator
  program and the reference model read past the list. Measured in `shapes/mapping-values.test.ts`: the short set
  settles with its slot empty; the long read settles with its value listed and stalls with a forged or a missing
  one; and an initializer's two inserts into an empty mapping, the MECE tags' shape, settle as one batch, with a
  chain that skips a key refused. That batch needed one rule in the declared-view walk: a record its segment
  declares twice, the predecessor the inserts share, is read at its first declaration on the input side, so its
  gap is proved against the view the batch started from, while the head still requires the later declaration to
  continue from the earlier one (`declared-view.test.ts`). The `put(string, string)` master now measures 5,983
  bytes and a long read's 4,578, under the per-input limit, so the 21,314 above no longer holds. Open: the long
  write (the composer lays out no value chunks), a long value read once per loop turn (refused by name), and a
  composer planner for a batch whose inserts share a predecessor: `layOutMappingWrites` chains inserts one after
  another, so it relinks a node the batch has only just created, which the head refuses as a record created
  twice. The test lays that batch out from the shipped encoders.
- [ ] R5 Dynamic `string`, `bytes`, `T[]`: consume every old chunk and create every new one. (partial: the master
  head now derives a body's chunk-run coordinate from the record block its own frame declares, and the machine
  accepts a compiled master whose entry appends to a `string` value — the old chunk consumed, the new one written,
  the length header updated — with a control refused when the run is undeclared or carries a value the code did not
  compute (`solidc/test/per-call/master/dynamic-state-record.test.ts`). The composer lays a run out as of
  `48e6b2d2c`: `planChunkRun` derives the value's header and chunk commitments through the shipped encoders and
  `layOutChunkRun` places the band one layout width past the block's declared records, locks every chunk with the
  value's own collection covenant and emits the record entry each one declares, and a plan built that way derives
  with the machine accepting the master that wrote the value (`solidcash/test/per-call/frames/chunk-run.test.ts`).
  **A write that changes the run's width is served** (`50b33ec71`, verified and widened this round): `layOutChunkRun`
  places kept, created and removed chunks, and the run that grows and the run that shrinks both settle — the
  `PER_CALL_CHUNK_UNPLANNABLE` refusal this note used to cite had already been removed when the planner landed, and
  `chunk-run.test.ts` now also drives a write that creates two chunks at once (`9f00b1286`). What is still
  unexercised is `bytes` and `T[]` runs. **A body that both touches a dynamic value and makes a call
  is now refused by name rather than crashing** (`110dfb155`): a call ends the segment the code before it runs in,
  so such a body declares its records per segment and has no single declaration the head can locate the run from -
  code generation used to ask for the direct convention's return-event operand and die with a stack-variable error,
  and the reason now names what cannot be located (`dynamic-value-call.test.ts`, with the write-without-a-call and
  the call-without-a-dynamic-value controls still served).
- [ ] R6 Mappings inside structs and nested mappings, through today's flattening and composite slots (spec 20).
  - The composite slot of a nested `m[a][b]` write is derived as of `7273d613e`: both derivation builders hash each
    target's keys in source order, concatenate the per-key hashes, hash them once more and mask the result the way
    the write path and the SDK's `keyToSlotMulti` do, so a two-key insert, its update and its read-modify-write are
    accepted by the machine (`solidc/test/per-call/master/composite-key-write.test.ts`). Several collections in one
    body derive as of `aaddcb5fc`: the record groups are read in one walk, in the order the frame declares them, and
    the created-record count is the body's region, so a body that writes `balances[to] += amount` and
    `allowances[owner][spender] -= amount` is accepted with a group declared for the other collection refused
    (`several-collections-write.test.ts`). **The removal is settled** at `07c80be46`, and the other way round from
    what was open: `delete balances[k]` lowers to the upsert of the value type's default, so the compiler never
    emits a removal's record group for the derivation to read — the group it declares is the default write's, whose
    written node the derivation does read, and the machine accepts both the update and the insert shape. Refused
    controls: a segment declaring the target removed and a written node declared under the empty view a splice would
    carry (`mapping-delete-write.test.ts`). Mappings inside structs and keys below the second level are
    unexercised.
- [ ] R7 Value pin (the spec's D2 rule) for mapping nodes and chunks, which carry none today (T39).
- Satoshi and token containers: not needed for the 66; deferred.

**Stream E: composer** (spec 5, 6, 10, 18).
- [x] E1 Resumable depth-first derivation from stop sites (A5): no fixpoint; a callee is derived first. The sites are published per entry by the compiler and read off the artifact (`2174295ca`), the driver resumes at the next published site and refuses a step that fills no witness, and the real host runs the compiled master twice per site — before and after the fill — counting the site filled only when the program strictly advanced, so a fill that moves nothing is a stall. A callee is derived before its caller resumes, which the tests assert as the order of completions. The composer lays out and assembles the draft itself as of `30aae727e`; the low-level host still accepts a hand-built run, and the plan's later items (reads of records, events, sends) are what remain of stream E.
- [x] E2 Witness assembly (A1), `M` (A2), block layout (spec 5), master and validator selection from the pools,
  several validators per transaction.
- [ ] E3 Reverts: the innermost reason, and custom errors with arguments, bubbled through callers; nothing is
  broadcast. **Partial** (`e0515bbf9`): a refused composition now reports the innermost frame's reason - the frame is
  found by asking the machine for a verdict on every frame of the draft, its depth read from the assembled caller
  references, and the report names the contract, the entry, the depth, the source line and the custom error the
  artifact publishes (name and declared parameter list); a nested revert reports the callee rather than the caller,
  both frames are reported when both refuse, and the provider is asserted to have seen no transaction. **The
  arguments are the gap, and it is the compiler's**: a custom error's values are not evaluated (`revert
  InsufficientBalance(100, 50)` is emitted as a failing verify and the compiler warns that error arguments are
  type-checked but not evaluated), so the report carries the error and its parameter list but not the values it was
  raised with. The helper is not yet wired into the derivation, which still stalls before it asks the machine -
  wiring it would change a stall semantics that several suites pin as the stall it is.
- [x] E4 Reads: view calls as `STATIC` `ROOT` frames off-chain, nested views, and reverts from reads. Landed
  (`4ab50885e`): `readPerCallEntry` classifies the contract's live records through the deployment layer, plans a
  static root over them, learns the returned bytes from the frame's own return comparison, derives the composition
  and decodes the value through the shipped decoders. The read is driven against a record an earlier accepted
  composition wrote (so it demonstrates real state), argument-passing reads work, and a nested read - a static call
  inside the read's root frame - lands with both frames and the callee's record accepted, the callee's value learned
  first and written into the caller's call record, and the derivation completing callee first. The walls are pinned
  rather than papered over: a read spends a validator UTXO (the head only admits a static frame with a validator
  reference), the validator reference's own verdict on a read-only root is a user-rooted rule and is asserted as
  such, a refusing read reports the machine's objection (without the `require` message, which the shifted failing
  instruction does not reach), the provider's transaction count is asserted unchanged, and the module refuses read
  entries that make external calls or touch mapping-backed records.
- [x] E5 Events to ethers logs and receipts: category to `addrV`, including nested frames. Landed (`85fc9c520`):
  the SDK reads the frames' own witness blocks as the census, matches each listed output to the event its covenant
  declares (anonymous events disambiguated by their covenant), decodes the commitment with the shipped decoder and
  reports a log whose address is the emitting contract's `addrV(category)` - not the covenant's lock - with the fields
  keyed by the artifact's parameter names and in declared order. Measured: a caller that emits and then calls an
  emitter that emits reports both logs with their own contracts' addresses, the nested one's five fields decoded from
  a value wider than 32 bytes; a composition that emits nothing reports nothing; an event output no frame lists is not
  reported, because the frames are the census (`test/per-call/receipt/`).
- [x] E6 A serialization queue for sends that could touch the same masters, validators or records. Landed
  (`perCallQueue`): a queue holds the tail of the sends that named each resource, starts a send once every resource
  it names is free, and resolves a send's plan only when its turn comes, so a later send is built against the state
  its predecessor produced. A send names its resources by the identity of the masters, validators and records its
  plan spends, or by the live outpoints when the caller knows them; a failing send settles its hold rather than
  wedging the queue. Measured (`test/per-call/queue/`): two sends enqueued against the same live master without
  awaiting either land in order, the second spending the first's own recreated output and the original outpoint
  gone; two sends with disjoint resources both complete; a refused broadcast leaves a disjoint send and its own
  overlap successor free to run, the successor spending the still-live master.
- [x] E7 Time: locktime fixed before derivation; strictly later block times in the mock. Landed (`47f38733c`): the
  composer's plan states the locktime and the sequence each participant runs under, fixes them once when the draft is
  built and exposes the fixed value, so every program the derivation builds is the same transaction and no frame can
  see the time change; the proxy's broadcast copies the derived locktime and per-participant sequences instead of a
  builder's defaults. Measured on a contract whose rule is a lock-check on the payment's own block: the payment's own
  block is refused with the machine's own message, the next block and the one after are accepted at the caller, the
  validator and the callee, and a plan whose locktime is already too early stalls the derivation (`test/per-call/time/`).
  **Named as not driven**: a frame that needs a different *relative* age per participant, and the genesis and read
  paths, which still fix a locktime of their own.
- [ ] E8 Simulation before funding: a send from an unfunded wallet reports its revert.
- [x] E9 Snapshots: `loadFixture` restores SDK state (nonce, look-ahead reservations, pools, caches). Landed
  (`takeReloadPoint`): a reload point pairs the provider's own snapshot - which already restores the chain and the
  signer nonces - with the state the SDK keeps beside it: the reservations a create look-ahead holds are put back to
  the set the point was taken over, and the master and validator ledgers a fixture reserves from are reset to their
  funded order. A bare rewind (or a reset) clears the reservations, through the mock network's reset hook, because a
  reservation names an outpoint of the chain it was made against. Measured (`test/per-call/deploy/reload.test.ts`):
  after a point is restored, a coin reserved since it was taken is selectable again, the coins it was taken over are
  still held, the ledger hands out its first master and validator again, and the chain is the point's own (including
  a UTXO the fixture's later work added); the same point restores twice; and a bare rewind drops the reservations,
  which is the control that the point is what puts them back.
- [x] E10 Listeners: `addListener` on the emitting address, including nested frames. Landed
  (`packages/solidcash/src/per-call/receipt/listeners.ts`): a registry keyed by the emitting address receives a
  composition's decoded logs through the shipped receipt, so a nested emitter's event reaches its own contract's
  handler and not the root's; a handler can be removed, and `deliverComposition` returns the logs it delivered.
  Measured (`test/per-call/receipt/listeners.test.ts`): a handler registered on one contract sees only that
  contract's event, the nested emitter's value (wider than 32 bytes) decodes into its own contract's handler, and a
  removed handler receives nothing.
- [x] E11 A test helper that deploys a contract with N masters and a validator pool, for the B, C, R and E tests
  before stream F lands. Satisfied by the shipped deployment rather than by a test-only helper, which is what this
  item asked for while it was still ahead of stream F: `MasterPoolDeployment` funds a named number of masters at the
  master lock and validators at the contract's own address, `deployMaster` answers a filled master and its address,
  and the focused suites deploy through them (`deploy/pool.test.ts` funds 12 masters and 2 validators and asserts the
  geometry, `deploy/deployment.test.ts` fills a master's slots, `deploy/genesis.test.ts` mints the pools and the
  records a constructor's frame computed). The shape the item wanted also exists in the composition tests as
  `shapes/harness.ts`'s `compileAndDeploy`, which compiles a multi-contract source per-call and deploys each named
  contract at its own seeded category.
- [x] E12 Pool exhaustion is refused before building, with `PER_CALL_POOL_EXHAUSTED` (spec 21). Tests: fill a master
  pool exactly, then ask for one more entry; a composition whose first root's contract has no validator.
- [x] E13 Derivation stalls are refused, with `PER_CALL_DERIVATION_STALLED` (spec 18). Tests: a step that fills no
  witness is refused; a 30-append derivation completes (no size cap).

### 4.3 Wave 2: deployment, proxies and acceptance tests (F and G, with R continuing)

**Stream F: deployment, pools, proxies, addresses** (spec 12, 19, 21, open point 13).
- [x] F1 Genesis with the master pool, and the validator pool at `addrV(cat)`; sizes as needed (D6). Landed with the
  constructor-frame derivation (`867cafe09`): the shipped deploy entry point selects the convention from the
  artifact, the master pool is minted at the master lock, the validator pool is one UTXO at the contract's own
  address, and one record per data NFT carries the commitment the constructor's frame computed. The marketplace
  fixture's four escrow contracts now deploy through it.
- [x] F2 Bake `addrX` into `code_X`; `getAddress()` returns `addrV(cat)`.
- [x] F3 CREATE look-ahead (`8bdd2e6d2`):
  - reserve genesis-eligible UTXOs: `CreateLookahead.reserve(n)` tops up to the number a caller says it will create,
    reusing the shipped genesis-funding selection, and a new reservation registry keeps a reserved coin out of that
    selection and out of the fan-out, so an address a caller was given cannot be spent by an unrelated send;
  - the `getCreateAddress` shim maps nonce `n + i` to the `i`-th reserved UTXO's category and answers
    `deployMaster(child, category, validatorProgram).address` - the same value the deployment produces, computed
    without broadcasting; measured equal to the deployed address for a child deployed through the shipped path;
  - an error pointing at the documentation when too few are reserved (`CreateAddressError`, naming
    `packages/solidc/docs/contract-creation.md`, which gains the section).
  - **Not done, and named**: no factory-level shim and no wiring into a real `new C(...)` execution or the composer
    (the `CREATE` frame itself remains spec open point 14), and `StatefulContract.deployFrom`'s own inline vout-0
    selection does not consult the reservation registry yet, so a `deployFrom` could still pick a reserved coin.
- [x] F4 User documentation in `packages/solidc/docs/contract-creation.md` on preparing genesis-eligible UTXOs.
  Landed: the document gains a section stating what makes a UTXO genesis-eligible (output index `0`, token-free,
  spendable, enough satoshis for the child's own genesis, and not reserved for another create), the two ways to
  satisfy it — an existing vout-0 coin, or the deploy's own fan-out of a fresh one from the account's spendable
  balances, which is what makes sequential deploys unbounded — and what to do when neither holds. It states how the
  reservations take part in both paths (a reserved coin is held out of the selection and the fan-out, which is what
  keeps a prediction true) and the order a caller follows: reserve, predict from the nonce, deploy with
  `getFundingUtxo(nonce)`, release.
- [x] F5 The built-in proxy: an `implementation` field, and a fallback that delegatecalls with the arguments and
  result unchanged. Landed (`5d4aeb6ed`): a caller does not write the proxy. `buildProxyArtifact` derives the source
  from the implementation's own published state layout - its fields in coordinate order, the struct definitions
  those fields name, and the fallback that forwards to the `implementation` field - compiles it with the shipped
  per-call compiler at the implementation's record geometry, and checks the generated layout against the
  implementation field by field, so a difference is a refusal naming the field rather than a contract that cannot be
  called after its first write. A stateful implementation deployed through the generated proxy settles, and the
  three unexpressible declarations (no `implementation` field, one that is not an address, a field of a type the
  layout does not publish) are refused by name. The proxy artifact becomes optional on the deployment entry point; a
  supplied one is held to the same agreement. **The `implementation` field still lives in the record** - the
  fallback reads it from there as the design says - and it is the *implementation* that must declare it, because a
  write rebuilds the record from the writer's own declaration. Putting the address in the proxy's *code* instead
  (a reserved slot the deployment fills) is what would remove that requirement, and it is not what landed.
- [x] F6 `deployProxy` in three transactions; `initialize` through the proxy; the `Initializable` flag in the proxy's
  records (title #8). Landed across `23a89c66b`..`39deb3656`:
  - the SDK builds the three transactions on the per-call path (`packages/solidcash/src/per-call/proxy/deploy.ts`):
    the implementation's genesis, the proxy's genesis carrying the implementation's address, and the proxied
    `initialize` as a delegate hop planned by the composer and derived by the shipped host;
  - **the write settles** (`61ef1f80b`, `39deb3656`). The state an entry leaves is not something a deployment can
    state in advance, so the hop is planned with a candidate and the value the entry actually computed is read
    back out of the machine - the entry closes its record against the declared view, the failed comparison leaves
    both operands, and the plan is rebuilt with the computed one (`deploy/declaration.ts`, shared with the
    genesis derivation). A frame's record-category check now resolves the *records' owner*: the executing input
    for a frame that owns its records, the caller's input for a delegate frame, which the witness block names as
    the caller reference. The head parks that index as one more tracked slot and the check reads it. Measured:
    the three-transaction deployment derives, every participant - both masters, the validator and the record - is
    accepted by the machine, and the record ends at the commitment the shipped encoder materializes from the
    written state with the implementation's address still in it, and a second `initialize` - offered the record the
    first one left, which is the `Initializable` flag set - is refused, with a control that the same plan offered the
    state the entry expects is accepted;
  - **a deviation is recorded for the record's shape**: a frame's write rebuilds the whole commitment from the
    fields *its own source* declares, so a proxy whose record does not declare the implementation's fields loses
    the address its fallback reads after the first call. A deployment therefore requires the two layouts to agree
    field by field and refuses a pair that does not (`39deb3656`, `records/partial-write.test.ts` measures the
    underlying property), and the proxy source declares the implementation's fields beside its own. The
    spec's section 19 wording ("its records hold the implementation's state layout plus one field") is realized
    this way; the built-in proxy of F5 is what removes the requirement by keeping the address in code;
  - **the SDK's ordinary entry point routes here** (`564d490e4`): `upgrades.deployProxy` reads the convention from
    the artifact's published descriptor and, for a per-call implementation, runs the same three transactions and
    returns the per-call deployment (`PerCallProxyDeployment`, not a `ProxyContract` - the doc comment and a branded
    overload say so). The proxy is the caller's or one generated from the implementation's declaration, and the
    options with no per-call equivalent (`initializer: false`, a non-Signer runner, a record-shaped seed) are
    refused with their own messages;
  - **the composer learns an unstated written view** (`3483fc768`): a plan may leave a segment's `viewOut` unstated,
    the draft replaces each unstated view with a placeholder, and a step that stops on one learns the value the body
    computed into it (the same channel the deployment layer reads). A stated view stays authoritative - a body that
    disagrees with it makes no progress and the stall is unchanged. A record whose output the plan leaves unstated
    carries the end of its declaration chain, recomputed each run, so a learned write reaches the output;
  - **the hop can be broadcast** (`a355347c3`): when the caller supplies its own funding, the derived hop is rebuilt
    on the outpoints the two geneses created - each participant found by enumerating its category rather than by
    assuming an index - with the assembled unlocking bytecodes and the derived outputs, the caller's own coin paying
    the fee and signing the transaction the root frame binds it to. One finding is recorded with it: the SDK's byte
    gate judges the hop against the production per-input limit and this composition's validator input is over it
    (10,567 against 10,000), which is what the composition profile exists for, so the hop goes to the provider's
    prepared-transaction path under that profile - on a real chain the per-call hop's input script is over the
    production limit whatever path builds it. The derived-only behaviour stays the default so callers that want the
    specification without sending it keep working.
- [x] (with F6) The validator program binds a delegate call's target to the callee's own code (`fe87a8b22`): the
  reference checker and the reason list named `V5_DELEGATE_TARGET_MISMATCH` but the shipped program did not emit
  it, so the machine accepted a delegate callee that was not the contract the caller's record named. The
  program-versus-derivation differential run keeps both directions clean.
- [ ] F7 The fixture's 14 transactions on the per-call path, with the predicted Unicrow addresses equal to the
  deployed ones.
- `split` and `merge` admin functions: not needed for the 66; deferred.

**Stream G: acceptance tests.** They are written test-first against the spec, and fail until their streams land.
- [ ] G1 Conformance suite (Track S0): the 21 reference contexts, EVM against per-call.
- [ ] G2 One dedicated test per spec section 23 row, kept as a regression guard:
  - [ ] Row 1: Reentrancy guard kept in storage and crossed by re-entry (`U._status`, `UC._status`).
  - [ ] Row 2: A static call reads state its caller's caller wrote moments ago.
  - [ ] Row 3: Which callee runs, and how many times, depends on that state.
  - [ ] Row 4: Calls inside data-dependent loops, including a revert mid-loop.
  - [ ] Row 5: Revert after state-changing calls (7 checks).
  - [ ] Row 6: Custom errors raised in a callee and re-raised by SafeERC20 assembly.
  - [ ] Row 7: `tx.origin` authorizes a nested call.
  - [ ] Row 8: `msg.sender` guards on proxy and CREATE-predicted addresses.
  - [ ] Row 9: `code.length` of a target.
  - [ ] Row 10: `address(this).balance` compared with 0 in `functionCallWithValue`.
  - [ ] Row 11: Low-level call, `bool` return decoding, revert re-raise.
  - [ ] Row 12: Struct and array arguments and returns with dynamic members; `uint16[5]`, `uint256[5]`.
  - [ ] Row 13: Events above 128 bytes, emitted by nested frames and asserted.
  - [ ] Row 14: Two JobEvents in one transaction (five transactions).
  - [ ] Row 15: Writes after an external call.
  - [ ] Row 16: 24-hour windows, the challenge period, strictly later block times, `Rated` timestamp equal to the review's.
  - [ ] Row 17: Snapshots and six mid-test reloads.
  - [ ] Row 18: Reverts from unfunded senders (7).
  - [ ] Row 19: Reverts from view calls (6).
  - [ ] Row 20: Un-awaited sends and matchers.
  - [ ] Row 21: Live event listener raced against 1 s.
  - [ ] Row 22: `ecrecover` on `"0x"`, 65 zero bytes (v = 0), a 20-byte value. Measured: all three are refused by
    name with the same reason as any other `ecrecover` body, so no invalid-input rule is exercised
    (`master/ecrecover-invalid-input.test.ts`).
  - [ ] Row 23: Records growing within one transaction and across 30 transactions.
  - [ ] Row 24: `string => string` mapping set and delete.
  - [ ] Row 25: Deep chains, four calls below M plus proxy hops, about 24 masters.
  - [ ] Row 26: A multi-megabyte master script in the mock (MarketplaceV1).
- [ ] G3 Hostile tests, one per threat (spec section 9); each must be rejected:
  - [x] T1: execute on a non-master UTXO (loose BCH at `lockA`, or a foreign minting NFT placed there).
  - [x] T2: forged `msg.sender` in standalone mode (`callerRef` pointing at a contract input, no validator).
    `hostile/standalone-caller-contract.test.ts`: a frame naming a contract's token-bearing input as its caller, with
    the frame's sender set to that lock, is refused by the machine; the attribution controls are that the same forge
    is refused whether the frame claims that lock or the user, and that a token behind a user lock is accepted, so the
    refusal is the caller's locking bytecode and not the token.
  - [x] T3: forged caller or call edge when a validator is present.
  - [x] T4: a validator left out where one is needed.
  - [x] T5: a fake validator (wrong derived lock, or no same-category mutable NFT).
  - [x] T6: a master pointing to a validator that does not list it.
  - [x] T7: split view across two validators (an edge or a contract's masters spanning them). `hostile/validator-split-view.test.ts`: a second validator input re-pointed at one of the contract's masters is refused by the derivation (`V3_MASTER_NOT_POINTING_HERE`) and by the machine.
  - [x] T8: forged arguments, selector or result.
  - [x] T9: reordered segments; stale read (`A.k` placed before `A.f`'s write). Ticked 2026-09-28. The stale read is
    refused (`hostile/stale-view.test.ts`, `V7_CHAIN_BROKEN`, machine refuses), and so is the reorder: swapping one
    master's two segments is refused by the derivation (`V7_CHAIN_START_MISMATCH`) and by the program at the validator
    input, which now follows the chains in the V6 order (`hostile/reordered-segments.test.ts`; see T30).
  - [x] T10: self-settlement claimed while a second master of the contract exists.
  - [x] T11: partial settlement of a multi-master contract.
  - [x] T12: a state UTXO spent outside settlement.
  - [x] T13: a forged or missing event.
  - [x] T14: a master or validator drained, duplicated, or not recreated.
  - [x] T15: an identity check keyed on a commitment instead of category, capability and lock.
  - [x] T18: a second A master claims `callerRef` = the first A master, which made no such call, and calls an
    `onlySelf` function. Also a master that names itself as caller or callee.
  - [x] T19: `callerRef` pointing at `DA` (a state UTXO at `stateA`) or at the validator.
  - [x] T20: the root user's input signed with `ALL|ANYONECANPAY`, `NONE` and `SINGLE`, one variant each.
  - [x] T21: `originRef` pointing at a second signed user in the transaction, at the root and on a callee.
  - [x] T22: a witness that tries to select a `catch` branch of `try this.g()` or `try b.g()` is refused; the call
    must succeed. **Ruled unreachable, recorded here**: the block carries no branch or status field and the compiler makes every
    catch dead code, so no witness can select a branch (general `try`/`catch` coverage exists in `solidc/test/TryCatch.test.ts`).
  - [x] T23: `this.g()` sees `address(this)`, and a caller-gated function reached through `this.g()` is refused. Use
    the EVM expectations recorded in the Log (2026-09-25 probe). Landed (`0e70219d6`): an inline call on `this`
    runs with `msg.sender == address(this)`, so `this.onlySelf()` is accepted where the source asserts that, and
    `this.ownerOnly()` called by the owner is refused, as the EVM refuses it; both are pinned by the identity toy
    (`toys/identity.test.ts`).
  - [x] T24: a funder P2PKH placed before the root user's input is never taken as `msg.sender` or `tx.origin`.
  - [x] T25: `this.g()` inline and `IA(t).g()` with `t == address(this)` on a second master give the same
    `msg.sender`. Closed on both spellings: the inline form carries `msg.sender = self` and agrees with the base-cast
    spelling (`Base(this).g()`), and a call through an interface value whose run-time target is the caller's own
    address now compiles and binds the record's target to the caller's frame `self`, so the callee `g` runs on a
    second master of the same contract and its `require(msg.sender == address(this))` holds — the same identity the
    inline form presents (`toys/identity.test.ts`). The controls keep the accept non-vacuous: the identical plan whose
    recorded target is another contract stalls at the caller, and the same guard reached from a different contract
    (an escrow refund) is refused.
  - [x] T26/T27: a proxied call whose `DELEGATE` callee is not the proxy's current implementation (the fallback's
    target), and a master presenting its code lock instead of its address. **Closed for the callee half**
    (`fe87a8b22`): `hostile/delegate-target-binding.test.ts` builds a real proxied composition whose DELEGATE callee
    is a second implementation compiled and deployed at its own category while the record names the first, and all
    three layers refuse it - the derivation names `V5_DELEGATE_TARGET_MISMATCH`, the reference names
    `THE_DELEGATE_TARGET_IS_NOT_THE_CALLEES_CODE`, and the validator program, which until this round did **not**
    enforce the rule, now refuses the same bytes; the control with the record's own implementation as callee is
    accepted. The wider shapes (a fallback beside ordinary entries, a receive, a delegatecall whose data is not the

- [ ] G4 Toys (neutral names), each settling with exact post-state, events and balances:
  - [x] **Y1 same-entry appends.** A `Hub` calls `Log.append(id, item)` k = 3 times; `append` pushes onto `items[id]`
    and emits. (The `publishJobEvent` shape.) Ticked 2026-09-27 (`7125f91b4`): the whole shape settles - one append
    at a time, two appends, and the three appends from an empty log **with one event per append**. Each case drives
    real source through the shipped compile, deploy, composer, derivation and machine, and the composed transaction
    is accepted at every contract input and the validator, with the created chunk's output, the log's header and
    each event's commitment read back through the shipped encoders. The body resolves the run's chunk through its
    own segment's declaration, which is what lets a created chunk sit in the trailing region after the events
    (`toys/same-entry-appends.test.ts`).
  - [x] **Y2 re-entry.** Moved twice. The wall was that a master making external calls could not carry a
    verified-mapping write at all; the refusal now names what it requires (`56411d2eb`): a body's write is
    admissible when it precedes every call the body makes, because the segment the write lands in is then the
    entry's own first segment, which the derivation reads, and the emission reads that segment rather than the
    one the frame ends in. **The design's writing half settles**: `Vault.release` rewrites the record and calls
    `Claimer.claim`, which re-enters the vault to mark the record claimed - the derivation completes the callee
    first, the machine accepts the two vault masters, the claimer and the validator, the record's output carries
    the commitment the shipped mapping planner computes, and each frame's declared view equals the plan's view
    for that frame's write. **The insert settles too** (`686dfeab6`): the failure was the body naming a created
    node by the direct layout band's coordinate while a composed frame's created records are trailing outputs
    the plan chooses, so the declared-record read found nothing and its non-emptiness guard failed; the insert
    arm now names the node by its position in the segment's declared list, and the creating frame, the
    re-entered frame, the claimer and the validator are all accepted, with the control (the created output
    carrying the creating frame's value instead of the value the last declaration ends at) refused by the
    validator. **An entry returning several values is admissible** (`e2a8e5424`, which this note predated): a
    tuple's canonical encoding is the concatenation of its values' own encodings, which is the same encoding a
    call's arguments go through, so the emission already built one blob and compared it as one item - only the
    admission refused the shape, and it now refuses a composite value only. **The rest of the toy's graph settles**
    (`b17c9b613`): `release` writes, calls `claim`, which re-enters the vault to mark the record claimed, pays twice
    through the token's `transfer`, and `release` emits what the claimer returned - ten participants accepted by the
    machine, the derivation callee-first, both records' chains read back through the shipped encoders, and a control
    whose record output no frame's declaration ends at refused. **Open, and measured**: a frame that *reads* a record
    it does not write cannot resolve the view its segment declares (the reading frame alone is refused, at
    `PER_CALL_DERIVATION_STALLED` on its own close), while the same composition's writing frames are accepted at the
    same coordinate - the reading frame's declared view comes back empty and the read helper's non-empty check fails.
    A related shape is pinned with it: a frame that reads a node an earlier frame created stalls the same way when the
    declared record's input is the no-index sentinel.
    - `Vault.release(id)` writes `recs[id].state = 1`, calls `Claimer.claim(id)`, and emits the returned amounts.
    - `claim` reads `vault.get(id)` and requires `state == 1`.
    - `claim` calls `vault.markClaimed(id)`, then `vault.pay(to, amt)` twice.
    - Each `pay` calls `Token.transfer`.
    - (The `release` / `claim` shape.)

    deployment really has - a stateful implementation whose entry writes the record it inherited from the proxy and

    F6): three transactions, `initialize` through the proxy, and the `Initializable` flag refused on the second call.

    listed event's category against the *executing* input, which is the wrong owner for a frame running on another

    collection and event addresses are owner-relative too. The wall this replaced was measured to its instruction
    (`518db903c`): the hop is
    sound (two plan faults in the driving test were fixed - the forwarded call data must be framed once, and the
    trailing outputs are a flat list), the derivation fills the proxy's call site and reaches the implementation, and
    the refusal is the **frame tail's event section**: it compares the event output's category against the *executing*
    input's, while the event belongs to the contract whose records the frame uses. Measured both ways - under the
    owner's category the tail reverts, under the executing input's the body reverts later - so no category satisfies
    both, and the fix has to make that section owner-aware (the owner the head parks is beneath the tail's own
    parkings, so it is a head-slot change rather than a local one). **Ticked 2026-09-28**: that clause is closed

  order, and the transaction's outputs are 5.2's set — but the box is unticked while the chains rule admits a

    the walk it runs, 3,201,534 of 5,452,000 with 41% of headroom (the Log row and the real-chain limits item below
    carry the numbers). The union census across the masters that run on a contract's records is emitted with the

    contract, and the multi-master controls that a master waives and the validator makes are in the validator
    suite. The chain end closed in the same round: the program's chains rule anchors each record's key, so the rule
    the derived order states (`V7_CHAIN_END_MISMATCH`) is in the list the differential holds.

  - [ ] **GO** requires:
    - 0 per-shape rules added;
    - derivation per entry;
    - no transcript parsing in masters.
  - [ ] **KILL** if any of these happens:
    - a toy needs a static per-shape recipe;
    - the settlement cannot do sorted-entry insert and delete generically;
    - re-entry needs a fixpoint across entries;
    - a shape in spec 5.4 needs a special case in contract code;
    - the compiler delta at I2 exceeds about 20K **net** lines across `packages/solidc/src`, reporting the new
      per-call subtree separately from the edits folded into the code that already existed. **User decision,
      2026-09-28**: raised from about 12K (restated to 15K by the implementer earlier that day) to about 20K, and the
      user is ready to drop the bound entirely: the delta is logic, not garbage. A mechanism that re-derives itself
      per shape, which the bound was meant to catch, is caught by the per-shape criteria above. The measured delta
      stays in the 4.5 measurements;
    - view mode needs a special case outside spec section 20's record kinds (`EVAL_PER_CALL_VS_KERNEL.md` sections 3
      and 6);
    - deriving the re-entry toy Y2 takes more than 5 s;
    - a V-check has no hostile test, or a threat row marked Critical (spec section 9) has no test showing it is
      rejected. The residual rows (T17, T32) are not Critical; T32's test records that it is accepted;
    - I2 is not reached within 4 weeks of the interface freeze (A8). One extension of 2 weeks is allowed if I1 is
      green and every GO criterion holds, with a written re-estimate in the Log.
  - [x] Record the gate decision in the Log, with a date and one paragraph of rationale.
  - **On KILL:** stop the swarm, write the gate report (which criterion failed, with its numbers), and return to the
    project owner. The options are then to redesign the failing mechanism, to cut scope, or to stop. Nothing
    continues by default.
  - **Stream R's bus factor.** R is the main risk and has one coder. Every R slice gets a second coder's review
    before it merges, and `MANIFEST.md` records R's state per slice, so another coder can take it over.

      at depth 4, derived callee-first, every contract input accepted, and the published sites pin the bound the
      fill rule gives (one distinct call per turn). Open: the marketplace's own `sendEscrowShare` arguments, which
      flow through SafeERC20 and `unicrow`'s value plumbing (P17, below). Behind it: L6 and L7 (#50-52, #59-62,
      #66).
    - **MS9, partial.** Sixteen broadcast transactions settle, and the constructor's event is no longer the wall
      (`53521a067`: `deployPerCallStatefulContract` mints one output per event the constructor's own frame
      computed — measured against the fixture's `MarketplaceV1.json`, `Initialized(uint64)` with `type(uint64).max`,
      byte-for-byte `encodeEventCommitment`, and an event payload off the computed value refused). Open: the
      CREATE-predicted, circular addresses the fixture deploys at (F3). Behind it: I3, so all 66.
    - **MS2, partial.** The store sits behind its own proxy and sees `msg.sender` = the root proxy's address
      (`8795cd234`), reached by a typed call now (`c9bda3383`: `IStore(storeProxy).append(tag, item)`), with the
      forwarding spelling and the framing it needed removed. Open: a store frame built from a run-time scalar stalls
      at the caller's segment close, and the appended struct's two `bytes` fields and the root's struct record
      with a `string[]` are not carried.
    - **MS3, partial.** A payable struct call at value 0, a write after a call, both events and `ecrecover` over an
      EIP-191 digest settle, after `b17fa3331` fixed the two shipped defects its first workarounds hid (a
      compile-time argument framed as its value instead of its push; a read-only frame closing a mapping node
      against the transaction's pair instead of its own declared view). Open: the real pull, one `transferFrom`
      call writing the allowance and both balance nodes, stalls at master `pull-1`'s first segment close
      (`OP_SPLIT`, split index 4) with the ledger shown not to be the fault; it is pinned beside the settling flow
      (`153ce42ea`).
    - **MS8, partial.** A struct with a `string[]` reads back through the shipped decoder, one frame per call.
      Open: a callee getter that takes a parameter is refused, because a read cannot resolve an argument from the
      caller's live records.
    - **MS10, partial.** Its refusals are pinned, including the compiler's warning that a custom error's arguments
      are not evaluated. Open: a custom error with arguments, re-raised through a low-level call (E3).
    - **Open across MS2-MS7.** P2, the typed call into a proxy, is served (`c9bda3383`): a forwarding fallback
      materializes its `input` from the frame's call-data band, so a hub's `IStore(proxy).append(item)` with a
      run-time argument settles and MS2's replica dropped the forwarding spelling. **P17 is served as of
      2026-09-28** (`693e8d441`): a payload at the call site, in a local, and through a helper's parameter - the
      `Address.functionCall` indirection - all lower to the typed call they denote, and a payload the compiler
      cannot name still refuses by name. P19: long values in chunks, partial (R5).
    - **Where the shapes stand, measured 2026-09-28 by compiling the project and reading every artifact's
      `composition.refusedEntries`** (78 refusals across the seven contracts when the census was first taken, by
      the reason the compiler publishes; **31** after the initializer inference stopped injecting the direct
      convention's proxy operand, **23** after the round that served the chunk-run and key shapes, and **13** after
      the round that served a call the source does not fix and a mapping write a call precedes):
      **52** of the original 78 were a proxy implementation's own entry, refused for the hidden
      `__internal__proxyMasterIndex` operand
      that Solidity-compatible initializer inference injects and a per-call delegate frame never reads
      (`MarketplaceDataV1` 37, `MarketplaceV1` 15 - the two roots that MS2, MS3, MS4, MS5 and MS8 all stand on);
      **16** were a body that reads or writes a dynamic value *beside* an external call, where the value's chunk run
      and the call's blocks share the frame's records (`MarketplaceV1`, mostly the job-post writers MS2 and MS5
      need); **3** were a
      dynamic-value chunk run whose width differs from the one coordinate the master leaves its bodies
      (`MarketplaceDataV1`'s two address-array getters and `FakeToken.__construct`); and **1** was a mapping write
      whose key is a ternary over values the body holds or a field of the state record rather than a bare parameter,
      the frame's sender or a compile-time value (`MarketplaceV1.refund`, `MarketplaceV1.refuseArbitration`,
      `Unicrow.pay` - MS3's pull). All of those are served now, along with the fifteen that were a call running
      conditionally, where the paths through the entry did not make the same number of calls. What the **12** that
      remain are, largest wall first:
      **8** are the raw low-level spelling of a call, which takes the direct path's return-event or value-transfer
      machinery that a per-call body does not emit (`MarketplaceV1`'s job-post writers, its releases and
      `payStartJob` - MS2's and MS5's own writers, so this is the wall those two replicas stand on - and
      `Unicrow.refund`/`sendEscrowShare`, whose send half is served and whose value-carrying low-level call is what
      refuses); **2** are a verified-mapping write behind a call whose occurrence the source does not fix, so the
      derived operands cannot follow the segment the write lands in (an imported token's `transferFrom`, and
      `Unicrow.pay` - MS3's pull); **1** is a keccak compute side-car whose
      co-spent inputs the master head does not locate (`MarketplaceV1.takeJob`); and **1** is a derived write key
      that reads an element of a dynamic state array (`MarketplaceV1.refuseArbitration`), which the derivation
      cannot emit where it runs. The send wall is gone: a listed send's lock may be a value the body computes, the
      body pins the lock and value it computed at the listed output, and a position the execution did not send at
      must carry the sentinel.
      The order the shapes take follows those counts, largest wall first.
      **Admission is gated on the T44 class** (the parallel worker's safety gate, 2026-09-28): no further shape is
      accepted until every record a frame declares in a segment is either checked against the code that declares it
      or required unchanged, and the shapes accepted after `6dd0bdfd8` — whose record positions move with the calls
      an execution actually made — are to be brought under that rule rather than exempted from it.
  - One neutral replica per flow shape MS1-MS10 in `solidcash/test/per-call/shapes/`, each settled through the
    shipped compile, deployment, composer, derivation and validator with exact post-state, events and balances.
  - Every primitive P1-P30 settles in its own focused test or inside a replica; the catalog's status column follows.
  - The I2 GO criteria hold for every replica: no per-shape rule, derivation per entry.
  - The catalog is closed: a title that fails later on a shape it lacks gets a neutral reproducer there first.
- I1, I2 and I2b are stated on the composition limit profile; real-chain limits are measured and gated in Phase R

  program is **10,484 bytes**, its own input's unlocking bytecode **10,568 bytes**, and the reference
  composition's other inputs' unlocking bytecodes are 273, 165, 17, 276 and 17 bytes, with a 25-byte P2PKH — so a
  master's witness block is two orders of magnitude smaller than the validator's transcript. Operation cost is
  5,248,052 of 8,487,200 (61.8%, 45,959 instructions). **The validator input is 568 bytes past the 10,000-byte
  consensus bytecode cap** and the program is 484 bytes past it; the case reports `accepted: true` only because
  the mock lifts the byte limits (`MOCK_CONSENSUS_LIMITS.maximumInputBytes`), and at `maximumInputBytes = 10_000`
  the machine refuses the shipped input. Rule 4.6 makes real-chain limits measured rather than gated until M2, so
